I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, even when there’s nothing specific going on at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the day, at least. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or discouraged, but it usually feels tied to whatever’s going on or what I’m worrying about. \n\nIf I’m answering that right, I wouldn’t say I’m just depressed all day for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things even when you're not at work, like when you're at home or trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do, more than I probably want to admit. It’s mostly work stuff that follows me home. Like I’ll be trying to relax and I’m still thinking about whether I missed a cycle-count deadline, or if some SAP issue is going to come back on me.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but since they put me in that acting workflow lead role, I’ve been tense even before I get to the warehouse. And then by the time I’m home, I’m so drained that I don’t really get myself to play basketball or sketch. I guess that counts as worrying a lot, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or notice symptoms like a racing heart, tightness in your chest, or trouble catching your breath, especially when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that in any big way. Like, I can feel tense or kind of keyed up when I’m worrying, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there with my heart racing or feeling like I can’t breathe.\n\nMaybe I get a little restless sometimes, but nothing that’s really stood out to me as a physical symptom, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do get a chance to play basketball or sketch, do you still find the same level of enjoyment or interest in those activities as you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I haven’t really paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nWhen I do actually get myself to do it, I think I can still enjoy it some… it’s not like I hate basketball or sketching now. Maybe it takes me longer to get into it than it used to, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still like those things. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even when you’re playing basketball or sketching, you don’t feel any pleasure or interest in doing them, like you used to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m slower to get into it or I’m kind of distracted, but I don’t think I’ve had a time where I was playing or drawing and felt absolutely nothing from it.\n\nI still get some enjoyment out of it once I’m doing it. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had days where you didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Like I’ll be trying to relax and I’m still thinking about whether I missed a cycle-count deadline, or if some SAP issue is going to come back on me.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but since they put me in that acting workflow lead role, I’ve been tense even before I get to the warehouse. And then by the time I’m home, I’m so drained that I don’t really get myself to play basketball or sketch. I guess that counts as worrying a lot, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or notice symptoms like a racing heart, tightness in your chest, or trouble catching your breath, especially when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that in any big way. Like, I can feel tense or kind of keyed up when I’m worrying, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there with my heart racing or feeling like I can’t breathe.\n\nMaybe I get a little restless sometimes, but nothing that’s really stood out to me as a physical symptom, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do get a chance to play basketball or sketch, do you still find the same level of enjoyment or interest in those activities as you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I haven’t really paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nWhen I do actually get myself to do it, I think I can still enjoy it some… it’s not like I hate basketball or sketching now. Maybe it takes me longer to get into it than it used to, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still like those things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're able to play basketball or do your sketches, do you feel the same sense of pleasure or interest that you used to, or does it feel more like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: I think… when I actually get going, there’s still some enjoyment there. I wouldn’t say it’s just going through the motions the whole time.\n\nMaybe it’s not as easy to feel into it right away as it used to be, but once I’m playing or sketching, I can still get some of that feeling back. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you’re playing basketball or sketching, do you feel the same pleasure or interest as you used to before all this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I still do, mostly. I mean, maybe it takes me a little longer to settle into it than before, but once I’m actually doing it, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s not like those things feel completely empty or anything. They still feel like things I like. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even when you’re playing basketball or sketching, you don’t feel any pleasure or interest in doing them, like you used to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m slower to get into it or I’m kind of distracted, but I don’t think I’ve had a time where I was playing or drawing and felt absolutely nothing from it.\n\nI still get some enjoyment out of it once I’m doing it. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had days where you didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I wasn’t exactly eager to start, or I was kind of in my head, but once I’m actually doing it, there’s usually still something there.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt no interest or pleasure at all during it. At least not that I can think of. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a loss, really. Like I said, sometimes I’m not as quick to get started, or I’m a little distracted, but when I’m actually playing or sketching, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI just don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my head is busy sometimes, not that those things stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s no specific work problem or deadline on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around anxious for no reason all the time or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel a little on edge and I can’t immediately pin it to a specific deadline, but I usually figure it’s probably connected to work or just life stuff in the background. So… I don’t know if that counts as worry without a specific problem in mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row where you didn't feel like doing anything you usually enjoy, even when things at work weren't particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not several days in a row. I’ve had times where I’m kind of tired or not feeling very motivated, but if work isn’t really pressing on me, I can usually still get myself to do something I normally like.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of stretch. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take much interest or pleasure in most of the things you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think it’s most things. I still care about stuff and I can still enjoy things once I’m doing them.\n\nThere are times I’m kind of distracted or less enthusiastic than usual, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. That wouldn’t be accurate, I don’t think.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like, I can feel tense or kind of keyed up when I’m worrying, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there with my heart racing or feeling like I can’t breathe.\n\nMaybe I get a little restless sometimes, but nothing that’s really stood out to me as a physical symptom, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do get a chance to play basketball or sketch, do you still find the same level of enjoyment or interest in those activities as you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I haven’t really paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nWhen I do actually get myself to do it, I think I can still enjoy it some… it’s not like I hate basketball or sketching now. Maybe it takes me longer to get into it than it used to, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still like those things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're able to play basketball or do your sketches, do you feel the same sense of pleasure or interest that you used to, or does it feel more like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: I think… when I actually get going, there’s still some enjoyment there. I wouldn’t say it’s just going through the motions the whole time.\n\nMaybe it’s not as easy to feel into it right away as it used to be, but once I’m playing or sketching, I can still get some of that feeling back. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you’re playing basketball or sketching, do you feel the same pleasure or interest as you used to before all this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I still do, mostly. I mean, maybe it takes me a little longer to settle into it than before, but once I’m actually doing it, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s not like those things feel completely empty or anything. They still feel like things I like. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even when you’re playing basketball or sketching, you don’t feel any pleasure or interest in doing them, like you used to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m slower to get into it or I’m kind of distracted, but I don’t think I’ve had a time where I was playing or drawing and felt absolutely nothing from it.\n\nI still get some enjoyment out of it once I’m doing it. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had days where you didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I wasn’t exactly eager to start, or I was kind of in my head, but once I’m actually doing it, there’s usually still something there.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt no interest or pleasure at all during it. At least not that I can think of. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a loss, really. Like I said, sometimes I’m not as quick to get started, or I’m a little distracted, but when I’m actually playing or sketching, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI just don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my head is busy sometimes, not that those things stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s no specific work problem or deadline on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around anxious for no reason all the time or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel a little on edge and I can’t immediately pin it to a specific deadline, but I usually figure it’s probably connected to work or just life stuff in the background. So… I don’t know if that counts as worry without a specific problem in mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row where you didn't feel like doing anything you usually enjoy, even when things at work weren't particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not several days in a row. I’ve had times where I’m kind of tired or not feeling very motivated, but if work isn’t really pressing on me, I can usually still get myself to do something I normally like.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of stretch. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take much interest or pleasure in most of the things you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think it’s most things. I still care about stuff and I can still enjoy things once I’m doing them.\n\nThere are times I’m kind of distracted or less enthusiastic than usual, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. That wouldn’t be accurate, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or low even when things at work and home are going fine, not tied to any specific problem or worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. If things at work and home are actually going okay, I usually don’t feel low out of nowhere.\n\nI mean, I have off days like anyone, but it usually feels like there’s some reason behind it, even if it’s just stress sitting in the background. I haven’t noticed it happening when everything’s basically fine. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I guess that counts as worrying a lot, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or notice symptoms like a racing heart, tightness in your chest, or trouble catching your breath, especially when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that in any big way. Like, I can feel tense or kind of keyed up when I’m worrying, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there with my heart racing or feeling like I can’t breathe.\n\nMaybe I get a little restless sometimes, but nothing that’s really stood out to me as a physical symptom, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do get a chance to play basketball or sketch, do you still find the same level of enjoyment or interest in those activities as you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I haven’t really paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nWhen I do actually get myself to do it, I think I can still enjoy it some… it’s not like I hate basketball or sketching now. Maybe it takes me longer to get into it than it used to, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still like those things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're able to play basketball or do your sketches, do you feel the same sense of pleasure or interest that you used to, or does it feel more like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: I think… when I actually get going, there’s still some enjoyment there. I wouldn’t say it’s just going through the motions the whole time.\n\nMaybe it’s not as easy to feel into it right away as it used to be, but once I’m playing or sketching, I can still get some of that feeling back. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you’re playing basketball or sketching, do you feel the same pleasure or interest as you used to before all this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I still do, mostly. I mean, maybe it takes me a little longer to settle into it than before, but once I’m actually doing it, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s not like those things feel completely empty or anything. They still feel like things I like. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even when you’re playing basketball or sketching, you don’t feel any pleasure or interest in doing them, like you used to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m slower to get into it or I’m kind of distracted, but I don’t think I’ve had a time where I was playing or drawing and felt absolutely nothing from it.\n\nI still get some enjoyment out of it once I’m doing it. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had days where you didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I wasn’t exactly eager to start, or I was kind of in my head, but once I’m actually doing it, there’s usually still something there.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt no interest or pleasure at all during it. At least not that I can think of. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a loss, really. Like I said, sometimes I’m not as quick to get started, or I’m a little distracted, but when I’m actually playing or sketching, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI just don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my head is busy sometimes, not that those things stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s no specific work problem or deadline on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around anxious for no reason all the time or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel a little on edge and I can’t immediately pin it to a specific deadline, but I usually figure it’s probably connected to work or just life stuff in the background. So… I don’t know if that counts as worry without a specific problem in mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row where you didn't feel like doing anything you usually enjoy, even when things at work weren't particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not several days in a row. I’ve had times where I’m kind of tired or not feeling very motivated, but if work isn’t really pressing on me, I can usually still get myself to do something I normally like.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of stretch. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take much interest or pleasure in most of the things you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think it’s most things. I still care about stuff and I can still enjoy things once I’m doing them.\n\nThere are times I’m kind of distracted or less enthusiastic than usual, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. That wouldn’t be accurate, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or low even when things at work and home are going fine, not tied to any specific problem or worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. If things at work and home are actually going okay, I usually don’t feel low out of nowhere.\n\nI mean, I have off days like anyone, but it usually feels like there’s some reason behind it, even if it’s just stress sitting in the background. I haven’t noticed it happening when everything’s basically fine. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel worried or anxious even when there’s nothing specific on your mind, like a general sense of unease that isn’t tied to work or anything else that’s happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that kind of free-floating way. Usually if I’m anxious, I can point to something that’s bothering me or something I feel behind on.\n\nI’m not saying I’m never uneasy, but if there’s really nothing going on, I don’t usually feel worried for no reason. I hope I’m understanding what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, even when there’s nothing specific going on at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the day, at least. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or discouraged, but it usually feels tied to whatever’s going on or what I’m worrying about. \n\nIf I’m answering that right, I wouldn’t say I’m just depressed all day for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things even when you're not at work, like when you're at home or trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do, more than I probably want to admit. It’s mostly work stuff that follows me home. Like I’ll be trying to relax and I’m still thinking about whether I missed a cycle-count deadline, or if some SAP issue is going to come back on me.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but since they put me in that acting workflow lead role, I’ve been tense even before I get to the warehouse. And then by the time I’m home, I’m so drained that I don’t really get myself to play basketball or sketch. I guess that counts as worrying a lot, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or notice symptoms like a racing heart, tightness in your chest, or trouble catching your breath, especially when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that in any big way. Like, I can feel tense or kind of keyed up when I’m worrying, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there with my heart racing or feeling like I can’t breathe.\n\nMaybe I get a little restless sometimes, but nothing that’s really stood out to me as a physical symptom, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do get a chance to play basketball or sketch, do you still find the same level of enjoyment or interest in those activities as you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I haven’t really paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nWhen I do actually get myself to do it, I think I can still enjoy it some… it’s not like I hate basketball or sketching now. Maybe it takes me longer to get into it than it used to, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still like those things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're able to play basketball or do your sketches, do you feel the same sense of pleasure or interest that you used to, or does it feel more like going through the motions?\n\nPatient: I think… when I actually get going, there’s still some enjoyment there. I wouldn’t say it’s just going through the motions the whole time.\n\nMaybe it’s not as easy to feel into it right away as it used to be, but once I’m playing or sketching, I can still get some of that feeling back. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you’re playing basketball or sketching, do you feel the same pleasure or interest as you used to before all this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I still do, mostly. I mean, maybe it takes me a little longer to settle into it than before, but once I’m actually doing it, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s not like those things feel completely empty or anything. They still feel like things I like. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even when you’re playing basketball or sketching, you don’t feel any pleasure or interest in doing them, like you used to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m slower to get into it or I’m kind of distracted, but I don’t think I’ve had a time where I was playing or drawing and felt absolutely nothing from it.\n\nI still get some enjoyment out of it once I’m doing it. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had days where you didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I wasn’t exactly eager to start, or I was kind of in my head, but once I’m actually doing it, there’s usually still something there.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt no interest or pleasure at all during it. At least not that I can think of. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, even when you’re actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a loss, really. Like I said, sometimes I’m not as quick to get started, or I’m a little distracted, but when I’m actually playing or sketching, I can still enjoy it.\n\nI just don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my head is busy sometimes, not that those things stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s no specific work problem or deadline on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around anxious for no reason all the time or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel a little on edge and I can’t immediately pin it to a specific deadline, but I usually figure it’s probably connected to work or just life stuff in the background. So… I don’t know if that counts as worry without a specific problem in mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row where you didn't feel like doing anything you usually enjoy, even when things at work weren't particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not several days in a row. I’ve had times where I’m kind of tired or not feeling very motivated, but if work isn’t really pressing on me, I can usually still get myself to do something I normally like.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of stretch. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take much interest or pleasure in most of the things you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think it’s most things. I still care about stuff and I can still enjoy things once I’m doing them.\n\nThere are times I’m kind of distracted or less enthusiastic than usual, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. That wouldn’t be accurate, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or low even when things at work and home are going fine, not tied to any specific problem or worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. If things at work and home are actually going okay, I usually don’t feel low out of nowhere.\n\nI mean, I have off days like anyone, but it usually feels like there’s some reason behind it, even if it’s just stress sitting in the background. I haven’t noticed it happening when everything’s basically fine. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel worried or anxious even when there’s nothing specific on your mind, like a general sense of unease that isn’t tied to work or anything else that’s happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that kind of free-floating way. Usually if I’m anxious, I can point to something that’s bothering me or something I feel behind on.\n\nI’m not saying I’m never uneasy, but if there’s really nothing going on, I don’t usually feel worried for no reason. I hope I’m understanding what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling a constant sense of worry or tension, even when you're not actively thinking about work or other specific problems?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it constant, no. I mean, I can feel tense when there’s something specific going on, but if I’m not thinking about work or some particular problem, I don’t usually have that worry sitting there by itself.\n\nAt least, I haven’t noticed it that way.
